    "newString": "  This boundary is independently corroborated: both supervised OBD and zero-shot frontier LLMs fail comparably at depths $\\geq 2$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$), establishing $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as a property of the data rather than the detection method (Section~\\ref{sec:why_kstar}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nWeb-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.\nEstimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.\nWhen models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.\nThis degradation arises in both \\emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \\emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nYet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically, discarding the generational depth dimension.\n\nThe gap cuts across three communities.\nBinary detection methods~\\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp, zawalski2026codec} produce no depth estimate.\nThe model collapse literature~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} characterizes recursive degradation at the macro level but provides no per-text depth estimation tool.\nData selection methods~\\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} ignore recursive depth.\nNo existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone, nor quantifies the boundary beyond which such estimation becomes infeasible.\n\nThe Data Processing Inequality guarantees that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\\Kstar$.\nWe empirically measure $\\Kempirical$---the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations---using 15 statistical features and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.\nWe operationalize $\\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \\geq 1$ where the bootstrap 95\\% CI for pairwise $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\\theta = 0.60$.\n\nOur contributions center on two empirical findings:\n\\begin{enumerate}[label=(\\alph*),nosep]\n  \\item \\textbf{$\\Kempirical = 2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$} (Cohen's $h{\\approx}0.20$) across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies at ${\\leq}$1.5B scale, with all 1v2 CI lower bounds exceeding $\\theta$ and all 2v3 CI upper bounds below $\\theta$ ($p \\leq 0.002$; Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n  Generation mode is a stronger determinant than model identity ($+$8.4\\,pp rewrite vs.\\ continuation within Qwen; $3.9$\\,pp spread across continuation-mode models).\n  The boundary is independently confirmed by convergent failure of both supervised OBD and zero-shot frontier LLMs at depths $\\geq 2$ (Section~\\ref{sec:why_kstar}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Model-free cross-model transfer}: 15 statistical features enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention across continuation-mode generators with scorer-invariant feature ordering ($\\rho > 0.90$; Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}), with surprisal-variance features dominating ($\\rho \\geq 0.85$).\n  Cross-scorer validation confirms that $\\Kempirical$ is governed by the generation process rather than the scoring model.\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\noindent As a downstream validation, $\\Kempirical = 2$ correctly predicts the strategy ordering in data filtering: binary curation at the $\\Kempirical$ boundary minimizes degradation most effectively (MMLU $0.5941$ vs.\\ base $0.6018$), while graduated ($0.5892$) and surprisal-based ($0.5894$) strategies provide no additional benefit (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).\n",
    "structuredPatch": [

        "oldLines": 7,
        "newStart": 21,

  "old_string": "(a)~$\\Kempirical = 2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Cohen's $h{\\approx}0.20$) across all tested configurations ($p \\leq 0.002$), implying a one-wash laundering effect---confirmed by convergent failure of both supervised OBD and zero-shot frontier LLMs at depths $\\geq 2$.\nGeneration mode is a stronger determinant than model identity ($\\Delta = 8.4$\\,pp), but rewrite-mode advantages are non-transferable (61--63\\% vs.\\ 80--92\\%).\n(b)~Model-free statistical features enable 80--92\\% cross-model transfer with scorer-invariant feature ordering ($\\rho > 0.90$), with surprisal-variance features dominating across model families ($\\rho \\geq 0.85$); cross-scorer validation confirms that $\\Kempirical$ is governed by the generation process rather than the scoring model.\nAs a downstream validation, $\\Kempirical = 2$ correctly predicts strategy ordering---binary curation minimizes degradation most effectively, while finer ordinal structure introduces noise rather than signal.",
  "new_string": "(a)~$\\Kempirical = 2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Cohen's $h{\\approx}0.20$), invariant across all tested configurations ($p \\leq 0.002$), implying a one-wash laundering effect.\nThis boundary is independently corroborated by the convergent failure of both supervised classifiers and frontier LLMs at depths $\\geq 2$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$), establishing $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as a property of the data rather than the detection method.\nGeneration mode is a stronger determinant than model identity ($\\Delta = 8.4$\\,pp), but rewrite-mode advantages are non-transferable (61--63\\% vs.\\ 80--92\\%).\n(b)~Model-free statistical features enable 80--92\\% cross-model transfer with scorer-invariant feature ordering ($\\rho > 0.90$), with surprisal-variance features dominating across model families ($\\rho \\geq 0.85$); cross-scorer validation confirms that $\\Kempirical$ is governed by the generation process rather than the scoring model, enabling depth estimation for text from unknown generators without retraining.\nAs a downstream validation, $\\Kempirical = 2$ correctly predicts the optimal curation granularity: binary filtering minimizes degradation most effectively, finer ordinal structure introduces noise rather than signal, and depth-agnostic methods (DSIR) fail entirely."
}

    "originalFile": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nWe presented the first systematic measurement of the generational depth discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$ for recursive synthetic text.\nTwo primary findings emerge:\n(a)~$\\Kempirical = 2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Cohen's $h{\\approx}0.20$) across all tested configurations ($p \\leq 0.002$), implying a one-wash laundering effect---confirmed by convergent failure of both supervised OBD and zero-shot frontier LLMs at depths $\\geq 2$.\nGeneration mode is a stronger determinant than model identity ($\\Delta = 8.4$\\,pp), but rewrite-mode advantages are non-transferable (61--63\\% vs.\\ 80--92\\%).\n(b)~Model-free statistical features enable 80--92\\% cross-model transfer with scorer-invariant feature ordering ($\\rho > 0.90$), with surprisal-variance features dominating across model families ($\\rho \\geq 0.85$); cross-scorer validation confirms that $\\Kempirical$ is governed by the generation process rather than the scoring model.\nAs a downstream validation, $\\Kempirical = 2$ correctly predicts strategy ordering---binary curation minimizes degradation most effectively, while finer ordinal structure introduces noise rather than signal.\n\nFuture work includes scaling to 7B+ models, extending to naturalistic multi-domain corpora, and developing model-agnostic neural probes.\nOur prompt-chain protocol isolates generation-level information loss in a controlled setting; establishing the retrain-chain $\\Kstar$ relationship, where training dynamics introduce additional degradation, is a natural next step.\nThe 100\\% synthetic filtering experiment characterizes an upper bound; mixed-corpus dose-response and base-vs.-instruct comparisons (partially controlled by our continuation-mode analysis) remain open.\n",

    "oldString": "  This boundary is independently corroborated: both supervised OBD and zero-shot frontier LLMs fail comparably at depths $\\geq 2$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$), establishing $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as a property of the data rather than the detection method (Section~\\ref{sec:why_kstar}).",
    "newString": "  This is independently corroborated: both supervised OBD and zero-shot frontier LLMs fail comparably at depths $\\geq 2$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$), confirming $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as a data property (Section~\\ref{sec:why_kstar}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nWeb-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.\nEstimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.\nWhen models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.\nThis degradation arises in both \\emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \\emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nYet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically, discarding the generational depth dimension.\n\nThe gap cuts across three communities.\nBinary detection methods~\\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp, zawalski2026codec} produce no depth estimate.\nThe model collapse literature~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} characterizes recursive degradation at the macro level but provides no per-text depth estimation tool.\nData selection methods~\\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} ignore recursive depth.\nNo existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone, nor quantifies the boundary beyond which such estimation becomes infeasible.\n\nThe Data Processing Inequality guarantees that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\\Kstar$.\nWe empirically measure $\\Kempirical$---the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations---using 15 statistical features and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.\nWe operationalize $\\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \\geq 1$ where the bootstrap 95\\% CI for pairwise $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\\theta = 0.60$.\n\nOur contributions center on two empirical findings:\n\\begin{enumerate}[label=(\\alph*),nosep]\n  \\item \\textbf{$\\Kempirical = 2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$} (Cohen's $h{\\approx}0.20$) across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies at ${\\leq}$1.5B scale, with all 1v2 CI lower bounds exceeding $\\theta$ and all 2v3 CI upper bounds below $\\theta$ ($p \\leq 0.002$; Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n  Generation mode is a stronger determinant than model identity ($+$8.4\\,pp rewrite vs.\\ continuation within Qwen; $3.9$\\,pp spread across continuation-mode models).\n  This boundary is independently corroborated: both supervised OBD and zero-shot frontier LLMs fail comparably at depths $\\geq 2$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$), establishing $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as a property of the data rather than the detection method (Section~\\ref{sec:why_kstar}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Model-free cross-model transfer}: 15 statistical features enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention across continuation-mode generators with scorer-invariant feature ordering ($\\rho > 0.90$; Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}), with surprisal-variance features dominating ($\\rho \\geq 0.85$).\n  Cross-scorer validation confirms that $\\Kempirical$ is governed by the generation process rather than the scoring model.\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\noindent As a downstream validation, $\\Kempirical = 2$ correctly predicts the strategy ordering in data filtering: binary curation at the $\\Kempirical$ boundary minimizes degradation most effectively (MMLU $0.5941$ vs.\\ base $0.6018$), while graduated ($0.5892$) and surprisal-based ($0.5894$) strategies provide no additional benefit (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nWe presented the first systematic measurement of the generational depth discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$ for recursive synthetic text.\nTwo primary findings emerge:\n(a)~$\\Kempirical = 2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Cohen's $h{\\approx}0.20$), invariant across all tested configurations ($p \\leq 0.002$), implying a one-wash laundering effect.\nThis boundary is independently corroborated by the convergent failure of both supervised classifiers and frontier LLMs at depths $\\geq 2$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$), establishing $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as a property of the data rather than the detection method.\nGeneration mode is a stronger determinant than model identity ($\\Delta = 8.4$\\,pp), but rewrite-mode advantages are non-transferable (61--63\\% vs.\\ 80--92\\%).\n(b)~Model-free statistical features enable 80--92\\% cross-model transfer with scorer-invariant feature ordering ($\\rho > 0.90$), with surprisal-variance features dominating across model families ($\\rho \\geq 0.85$); cross-scorer validation confirms that $\\Kempirical$ is governed by the generation process rather than the scoring model, enabling depth estimation for text from unknown generators without retraining.\nAs a downstream validation, $\\Kempirical = 2$ correctly predicts the optimal curation granularity: binary filtering minimizes degradation most effectively, finer ordinal structure introduces noise rather than signal, and depth-agnostic methods (DSIR) fail entirely.\n\nFuture work includes scaling to 7B+ models, extending to naturalistic multi-domain corpora, and developing model-agnostic neural probes.\nOur prompt-chain protocol isolates generation-level information loss in a controlled setting; establishing the retrain-chain $\\Kstar$ relationship, where training dynamics introduce additional degradation, is a natural next step.\nThe 100\\% synthetic filtering experiment characterizes an upper bound; mixed-corpus dose-response and base-vs.-instruct comparisons (partially controlled by our continuation-mode analysis) remain open.\n",

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	We presented the first systematic measurement of the generational depth discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ for recursive synthetic text.
5	Two primary findings emerge:
6	(a)~$\Kempirical = 2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ (Cohen's $h{\approx}0.20$), invariant across all tested configurations ($p \leq 0.002$), implying a one-wash laundering effect.
7	Convergent failure of both supervised classifiers and frontier LLMs at depths $\geq 2$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$) independently corroborates this as a data property rather than a method limitation.
8	Generation mode is a stronger determinant than model identity ($\Delta = 8.4$\,pp), but rewrite-mode advantages are non-transferable (61--63\% vs.\ 80--92\%).
9	(b)~Model-free statistical features enable 80--92\% cross-model transfer with scorer-invariant feature ordering ($\rho > 0.90$), with surprisal-variance features dominating across model families ($\rho \geq 0.85$); cross-scorer validation confirms that $\Kempirical$ is governed by the generation process rather than the scoring model, enabling depth estimation for text from unknown generators without retraining.
10	As a downstream validation, $\Kempirical = 2$ correctly predicts the optimal curation granularity: binary filtering minimizes degradation most effectively; finer ordinal structure and depth-agnostic methods (DSIR) provide no benefit.
11	
12	Future work includes scaling to 7B+ models, extending to naturalistic multi-domain corpora, and developing model-agnostic neural probes.
13	Our prompt-chain protocol isolates generation-level information loss in a controlled setting; establishing the retrain-chain $\Kstar$ relationship, where training dynamics introduce additional degradation, is a natural next step.
14	The 100\% synthetic filtering experiment characterizes an upper bound; mixed-corpus dose-response and base-vs.-instruct comparisons (partially controlled by our continuation-mode analysis) remain open.
15	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_011Ue9aDXRzngyJ8M1V3zurT`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	Web-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.
5	Estimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.
6	When models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.
7	This degradation arises in both \emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
8	Yet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically, discarding the generational depth dimension.
9	
10	The gap cuts across three communities.
11	Binary detection methods~\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp, zawalski2026codec} produce no depth estimate.
12	The model collapse literature~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} characterizes recursive degradation at the macro level but provides no per-text depth estimation tool.
13	Data selection methods~\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} ignore recursive depth.
14	No existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone, nor quantifies the boundary beyond which such estimation becomes infeasible.
15	
16	The Data Processing Inequality guarantees that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\Kstar$.
17	We empirically measure $\Kempirical$---the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations---using 15 statistical features and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.
18	We operationalize $\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \geq 1$ where the bootstrap 95\% CI for pairwise $\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\theta = 0.60$.
19	
20	Our contributions center on two empirical findings:
21	\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),nosep]
22	  \item \textbf{$\Kempirical = 2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$} (Cohen's $h{\approx}0.20$) across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies at ${\leq}$1.5B scale, with all 1v2 CI lower bounds exceeding $\theta$ and all 2v3 CI upper bounds below $\theta$ ($p \leq 0.002$; Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
23	  Generation mode is a stronger determinant than model identity ($+$8.4\,pp rewrite vs.\ continuation within Qwen; $3.9$\,pp spread across continuation-mode models).
24	  This is independently corroborated: both supervised OBD and zero-shot frontier LLMs fail comparably at depths $\geq 2$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$), confirming $\Kempirical{=}2$ as a data property (Section~\ref{sec:why_kstar}).
25	  \item \textbf{Model-free cross-model transfer}: 15 statistical features enable 80--92\% accuracy retention across continuation-mode generators with scorer-invariant feature ordering ($\rho > 0.90$; Section~\ref{sec:transfer}), with surprisal-variance features dominating ($\rho \geq 0.85$).
26	  Cross-scorer validation confirms that $\Kempirical$ is governed by the generation process rather than the scoring model.
27	\end{enumerate}
28	
29	\noindent As a downstream validation, $\Kempirical = 2$ correctly predicts the strategy ordering in data filtering: binary curation at the $\Kempirical$ boundary minimizes degradation most effectively (MMLU $0.5941$ vs.\ base $0.6018$), while graduated ($0.5892$) and surprisal-based ($0.5894$) strategies provide no additional benefit (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
30	
```

    "newString": "Across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies (${\\leq}1.5$B scale), $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Cohen's $h{\\approx}0.20$), invariant across all 8 conditions:",
    "originalFile": "% EMNLP 2026 Main — ARR May 2026\n% Generational Depth Contamination\n\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}\n\\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}\n\\usepackage{microtype}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{enumitem}\n\\usepackage{subcaption}\n\\usepackage{placeins}\n\n\\newcommand{\\Kstar}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Kempirical}{{K^*_{\\text{empirical}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\Ktheory}{{K^*_{\\text{theory}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\auc}{\\text{AUC}}\n\\newcommand{\\jsd}{\\text{JSD}}\n\n\\title{Generational Depth Estimation of Recursive Synthetic Text:\\\\Measuring the Discrimination Boundary}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n% === Abstract ===\n\\begin{abstract}\nWeb-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure the discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$---the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain distinguishable---using 15 statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.\nAcross 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies (${\\leq}1.5$B scale), $\\Kempirical{=}2$, invariant at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Cohen's $h{\\approx}0.20$) across all 8 conditions: a single generation step is reliably detectable, but the second renders text statistically indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content---a \\emph{one-wash laundering} effect.\nModel-free statistical features enable 80--92\\% cross-model transfer with scorer-invariant feature ordering ($\\rho > 0.90$), and cross-scorer validation confirms that $\\Kempirical$ is governed by the generation process rather than the scoring model.\nBoth supervised classification and zero-shot frontier LLMs fail comparably at depths ${\\geq}2$ (recall 14--22\\% vs.\\ 8--38\\%), confirming $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as a property of the data rather than a method limitation.\nDownstream, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts that binary curation minimizes degradation most effectively among all tested strategies.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n% === Section 1: Introduction ===\n\\input{introduction}\n\n% === Section 2: Related Work ===\n\\input{related_work}\n\n% === Section 3: Method ===\n\\input{method}\n\n% === Section 4: Experiments ===\n\\input{experiments_c1}\n\n% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===\n\\input{experiments_c3}\n\n% === Section 6: Discussion ===\n\\input{discussion}\n\n% === Section 7: Conclusion ===\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur findings are bounded by (1)~model scale ($\\leq$1.5B), (2)~prompt-chain rather than retrain-chain protocols (our $\\Kempirical$ is a conservative upper bound on retrain-chain $\\Kstar$ under monotonic-degradation assumptions), (3)~English C4 text only, (4)~four decoding strategies, (5)~threshold sensitivity ($\\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$ depending on configuration; all four configurations yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at our chosen $\\theta{=}0.60$), (6)~marginal boundary (Qwen-continuation 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\\theta$ by only 0.0015; cross-scorer validation with both Pythia and OLMo scorers drops this condition to $\\Kempirical{=}1$, Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}), (7)~the 15D feature set yields a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher), (8)~the neural probe boundary is model-dependent ($\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}}{=}3$ for Qwen only; $2$ for Pythia/OLMo), likely reflecting self-familiarity rather than a universal signal, and (9)~while overall feature rankings are highly consistent across models ($\\rho \\geq 0.85$), depth-pair-level agreement weakens at the 1v2 boundary ($\\tau \\approx 0.37$), indicating that per-feature contributions at the critical boundary vary across models.\nDetailed analysis appears in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.\n\n% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nGenerational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing---positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.\nOur experiments use the publicly available C4 corpus and do not involve personally identifiable information.\nA potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\\auc > 0.99$ at 0v1) remains robust.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\input{appendix}\n\\input{appendix_extended}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

[Director 指令] [Patch] exp-004 7B cross-scale 结果出来了——**K*=2 confirmed at 7B**。使用之前准备的 Scenario A 模板整合：

**核心数据**：
- Qwen-7B: AUC 0v1=0.9644, AUC 1v2=0.6178, AUC 2v3=0.5460, K*=2
- 5-fold CV 全部 5 折无分歧（1v2 最低 0.6053 > θ=0.60, 2v3 最高 0.5491 < 0.56）

**整合要点**：
1. 6 处 "≤1.5B" → "1.5B–7B scale"（按模板标记位置）
2. Table 1 加 Qwen-7B 行，Fig 2 加 7B 线
3. method.tex "four configurations" → "five configurations"
4. §4.1 加一句 scale extension + 5-fold robustness

**Reviewer 观察需处理**：
- 0v1 AUC 下降 ~3pp（0.9644 vs >0.99）：Discussion 提及，7B 文本更接近人类所以 binary detection 更难，暗示更大 scale 0v1 可能继续下降
- JSD 微非单调（2v3 vs 3v4 差 0.000023）：用 "approximately monotonic" 而非 "strictly monotonic"
- K*=2 现在是 **9/9 条件**（原 8 + 7B），"8 conditions" 全部改为 "9 conditions"

编译确认 18 页。

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We evaluate generational depth estimation on four model configurations:
5	Pythia-1.4B and OLMo-1B in continuation mode, and Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct in both rewrite and continuation modes.
6	All experiments use the OBD-LightGBM classifier with the 15-dimensional feature set (Section~\ref{sec:method}), with 5{,}000 texts per depth level (generations 0--5).
7	
8	%% ====================================================================
9	\subsection{$\Kempirical = 2$ Across All Configurations}
10	\label{sec:kstar_boundary}
11	
12	\begin{table*}[t]
13	\centering
14	\small
15	\caption{Pairwise $\auc$ for adjacent depth pairs with bootstrap 95\% CIs and permutation $p$-values for boundary rows.
16	$\Kempirical = 2$ requires 1v2 $\text{CI}_\text{low} > \theta{=}0.60$ and 2v3 $\text{CI}_\text{high} < \theta$.}
17	\label{tab:kstar_ci}
18	\begin{tabular}{lcccccccc}
19	\toprule
20	 & \multicolumn{2}{c}{Qwen-rw} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{Pythia} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{OLMo} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{Qwen-cont} \\
21	\cmidrule(lr){2-3}\cmidrule(lr){4-5}\cmidrule(lr){6-7}\cmidrule(lr){8-9}
22	 & $\auc$ & 95\% CI & $\auc$ & 95\% CI & $\auc$ & 95\% CI & $\auc$ & 95\% CI \\
23	\midrule
24	0v1 & .9956 & & .9932 & & .9970 & & .9971 & \\
25	1v2 & .6955 & [.686,\,.704]\rlap{$^*$} & .6539 & [.645,\,.663]\rlap{$^*$} & .6145 & [.605,\,.625]\rlap{$^*$} & .6112 & [.602,\,.621]\rlap{$^*$} \\
26	2v3 & .5893 & [.580,\,.599] & .5514 & [.542,\,.563] & .5496 & [.540,\,.560] & .5609 & [.551,\,.571] \\
27	3v4 & .5435 & & .5152 & & .5091 & & .5409 & \\
28	4v5 & .5340 & & .5136 & & .5045 & & .5141 & \\
29	\bottomrule
30	\multicolumn{9}{l}{\footnotesize $^*$Permutation $p = 0.002$ for all 1v2 comparisons.}
31	\end{tabular}
32	\end{table*}
33	
34	\begin{figure}[t]
35	\centering
36	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_auc_decay.pdf}
37	\caption{Pairwise $\auc$ decay across depth pairs for model-mode configurations (solid) and decoding strategies (dashed). The horizontal dashed line marks $\theta = 0.60$.}
38	\label{fig:auc_decay}
39	\end{figure}
40	
41	Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci} and Figure~\ref{fig:auc_decay} show a consistent pattern: 0v1 discrimination is near-perfect ($\auc > 0.99$), followed by a sharp ${\sim}$30\,pp drop at 1v2 ($\auc$: 0.6112--0.6955), with 2v3 falling below $\theta$ (0.5496--0.5893) and deeper pairs converging toward chance.
42	All four 1v2 CI lower bounds exceed $\theta$ (most marginal: Qwen-continuation at 0.6019), and all four 2v3 CI upper bounds fall below $\theta$, with permutation $p = 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons.
43	This establishes $\Kempirical = 2$ as invariant across all 8 tested conditions (4 model-mode $\times$ 4 decoding; Table~\ref{tab:s2_decoding}).
44	The sharp step-function pattern reflects the concentration of information loss: human text exhibits heavy-tailed, bursty surprisal patterns that are substantially altered by even a single autoregressive pass.
45	Subsequent generations operate on already-smoothed distributions, yielding progressively smaller marginal changes that fall below detectability within two steps.
46	
47	The 1v2 $\auc$ varies across models: Qwen-rewrite (0.6955) $>$ Pythia (0.6539) $>$ OLMo (0.6145) $\approx$ Qwen-continuation (0.6112).
48	The spread due to generation mode within a single model ($\Delta = 8.4$\,pp) exceeds the spread across models in the same mode ($\Delta = 3.9$\,pp), suggesting generation protocol is a stronger determinant of detectability than model identity.
49	
50	\begin{figure}[t]
51	\centering
52	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_kstar_theta.pdf}
53	\caption{$\Kempirical$ as a function of $\theta$ across four model-mode configurations, computed using the 95\% CI lower bound criterion (Eq.~\ref{eq:kstar}). The dashed line marks the operational threshold $\theta{=}0.60$ used throughout, where all configurations yield $\Kempirical{=}2$. Qwen-rewrite yields the highest $\Kempirical$ at all $\theta$; at $\theta{=}0.70$, $\Kempirical{=}1$ universally.}
54	\label{fig:kstar_theta}
55	\end{figure}
56	
57	Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta} shows $\Kempirical$ as a function of $\theta$ (using 95\% CI lower bounds): at $\theta = 0.55$, $\Kempirical = 3$ for Qwen-rewrite and Qwen-continuation while Pythia and OLMo remain at $\Kempirical = 2$; at $\theta = 0.65$, all continuation modes drop to $\Kempirical = 1$ while rewrite retains $\Kempirical = 2$; at $\theta = 0.70$, $\Kempirical = 1$ universally.
58	All four decoding configurations (greedy, nucleus, $T{=}0.70$, $T{=}1.2$) likewise yield $\Kempirical = 2$ at $\theta = 0.60$, with 1v2 $\auc$ in $[0.6866, 0.7039]$ (Table~\ref{tab:s2_decoding}).
59

```text
33	\begin{abstract}
34	Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.
35	We measure the discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$---the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain distinguishable---using 15 statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.
36	Across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies (${\leq}1.5$B scale), $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ (Cohen's $h{\approx}0.20$), invariant across all 8 conditions: a single generation step is reliably detectable, but the second renders text statistically indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content---a \emph{one-wash laundering} effect.
37	Model-free statistical features enable 80--92\% cross-model transfer with scorer-invariant feature ordering ($\rho > 0.90$), and cross-scorer validation confirms that $\Kempirical$ is governed by the generation process rather than the scoring model.
38	Both supervised classification and zero-shot frontier LLMs fail comparably at depths ${\geq}2$ (recall 14--22\% vs.\ 8--38\%), confirming $\Kempirical{=}2$ as a property of the data rather than a method limitation.
39	Downstream, $\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts that binary curation minimizes degradation most effectively among all tested strategies.
40	\end{abstract}
```

63	% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===
64	\section*{Limitations}
65	
66	Our findings are bounded by (1)~model scale ($\leq$1.5B), (2)~prompt-chain rather than retrain-chain protocols (our $\Kempirical$ is a conservative upper bound on retrain-chain $\Kstar$ under monotonic-degradation assumptions), (3)~English C4 text only, (4)~four decoding strategies, (5)~threshold sensitivity ($\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\theta{=}0.70$ depending on configuration; all four configurations yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ at our chosen $\theta{=}0.60$), (6)~marginal boundary (Qwen-continuation 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\theta$ by only 0.0015; cross-scorer validation with both Pythia and OLMo scorers drops this condition to $\Kempirical{=}1$, Section~\ref{sec:transfer}), (7)~the 15D feature set yields a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher), (8)~the neural probe boundary is model-dependent ($\Kempirical^{\text{neural}}{=}3$ for Qwen only; $2$ for Pythia/OLMo), likely reflecting self-familiarity rather than a universal signal, and (9)~while overall feature rankings are highly consistent across models ($\rho \geq 0.85$), depth-pair-level agreement weakens at the 1v2 boundary ($\tau \approx 0.37$), indicating that per-feature contributions at the critical boundary vary across models.
67	Detailed analysis appears in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.
68	
69	% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===

```text
38	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
39	\label{sec:experimental_setup}
40	
41	We evaluate four configurations spanning three model families (Table~\ref{tab:setup}): Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation modes), Pythia-1.4B and OLMo-1B (continuation).
42	Each uses 5{,}000 C4 seeds truncated to 256 tokens, recursively generated for $K{=}5$ iterations (30{,}000 documents per configuration).
43	Default decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; decoding ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.
44	Continuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.
45	Evaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\auc$ as primary metric.
46	
47	\begin{table}[t]
48	\centering
49	\small

      "content": "\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:experimental_setup}\n\nWe evaluate four configurations spanning three model families (Table~\\ref{tab:setup}): Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation modes), Pythia-1.4B and OLMo-1B (continuation).\nEach uses 5{,}000 C4 seeds truncated to 256 tokens, recursively generated for $K{=}5$ iterations (30{,}000 documents per configuration).\nDefault decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; decoding ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.\nContinuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.\nEvaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\\auc$ as primary metric.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Experimental configurations.}\n\\label{tab:setup}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nPythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nOLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01FZpqa7SzX896JLbegyLVTc`</summary>

```text
0	\section{Extended Discussion}
1	\label{sec:extended_discussion}
2	
3	\subsection{Theoretical Implications of $\Kempirical = 2$}
4	
5	The Data Processing Inequality guarantees the existence of a finite $\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations become indistinguishable (Section~\ref{sec:formulation}).
6	Our empirical result---$\Kempirical = 2$ consistently across all 8 tested configurations---establishes that this boundary is remarkably shallow at the $\leq$1.5B scale.
7	
8	The shallowness is explained by the rate of information loss.
9	The 0v1 boundary is near-trivially separable ($\auc > 0.99$), reflecting the large distributional gap between human and first-generation text.
10	At 1v2, $\auc$ drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp (range: 0.6112--0.7039 across all conditions), indicating that the majority of discriminative signal is consumed in the first generation step.
11	By 2v3, $\auc$ falls to 0.5496--0.5917---below $\theta = 0.60$---and deeper pairs converge toward chance.
12	
13	This pattern aligns with model collapse findings.
14	Shumailov et al.~\cite{shumailov2024collapse} observed that tail-distribution degradation is most acute in the earliest generations.
15	Dohmatob et al.~\cite{dohmatob2024model} derived scaling laws demonstrating predictable tail probability mass loss.
16	Alemohammad et al.~\cite{alemohammad2024selfconsuming} proved convergence to a fixed point; $\Kempirical = 2$ indicates that operationally, this approach is rapid enough that adjacent-generation distinctions collapse within two steps.
17	
18	The consistency across decoding strategies merits emphasis.
19	Greedy, nucleus ($p{=}0.9$), $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$ all yield $\Kempirical = 2$ (Table~\ref{tab:s2_decoding}), with 1v2 $\auc$ in $[0.6866, 0.7039]$.
20	Higher decoding entropy modestly increases signal strength but does not shift the boundary, suggesting that $\Kempirical$ is governed by the information bottleneck of the generation process rather than sampling algorithm properties.
21	
22	\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer Implications}
23	
24	The cross-model transfer results (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}) indicate that the relative importance of depth features is largely text-intrinsic: feature rankings are highly correlated across scorers ($\rho > 0.90$), and cross-model classification retains 80--92\% accuracy (Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo: 90--92\%; broader continuation-mode pairs: 80--92\%).
25	However, absolute discriminability at the $\Kempirical$ boundary is scorer-sensitive---alternative scorers reduce Qwen-continuation $\Kempirical$ from 2 to 1, while rewrite-mode $\Kempirical = 2$ is preserved (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}).
26	At 0v1, cross-model $\auc$ exceeds 0.96 for all pairs, confirming fully transferable binary contamination detection.
27	
28	A single depth classifier trained on one model's outputs can be deployed for texts from other models without retraining---extending utility to open-web settings where the generating model is unknown.
29	
30	Two caveats: (1)~a nontrivial model-specific residual persists despite high transfer, and (2)~Qwen-continuation$\leftrightarrow$OLMo retention (80--85\%) is lower than Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo (90--92\%), indicating that model family similarity modulates transfer even among continuation-mode configurations.
31	
32	\begin{table}[t]
33	\centering
34	\caption{Cross-scorer validation on Qwen-generated text. Alternative scorers (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B) preserve feature ranking ($\rho > 0.90$) and rewrite-mode $\Kempirical = 2$, but reduce continuation-mode $\Kempirical$ from 2 to 1. $\dagger$: CI lower bound below $\theta{=}0.60$. Underlined: $\auc < \theta$.}
35	\label{tab:crossscorer}
36	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\input{tables/tab_crossscorer}}
37	\end{table}
38	
39	\subsection{Feature Importance Across Decoding Strategies}
40	
41	While $\Kempirical = 2$ is consistent across decoding strategies, feature importance ordering varies.
42	Greedy and $T{=}0.7$ share highly correlated rankings (Spearman $\rho = 0.914$), with vocabulary diversity measures dominating.
43	Greedy versus $T{=}1.2$ yields lower correlation ($\rho = 0.53$, $p = 0.041$; suggestive but nonsignificant after Bonferroni correction).
44	$T{=}1.2$ promotes \texttt{hapax\_ratio}, \texttt{ttr}, and \texttt{surp\_skew} to top ranks; \texttt{freq\_entropy} rises from rank~10 (greedy) to rank~5.
45	
46	The \emph{existence} of the depth boundary is robust, but the \emph{optimal feature subset} depends on sampling strategy.
47	A general-purpose classifier should incorporate features from multiple groups.
48	
49	\subsection{Scale Considerations}
50	
51	Our $\Kempirical = 2$ at $\leq$1.5B constitutes a methodological lower bound.
52	All 15 features are computed from surface-level token statistics without model internals, and cross-model transfer (80--92\%) demonstrates methodology generalization across architectures.
53	
54	Scaling to larger models presents two outcomes: if $\Kempirical > 2$ at 7B+ (as scaling laws suggest~\cite{dohmatob2024model}), depth contamination becomes more insidious and our framework more necessary; if $\Kempirical = 2$ persists, our conclusions generalize directly.
55	The high cross-model transfer across three architecturally distinct families provides indirect evidence that depth signals are not tied to a specific pretraining paradigm.
56	
57	\subsection{Cross-Model Neural Probe Analysis}
58	\label{sec:extended_neural_probe}
59

broader continuation-mode pairs: 80--92\\%).\nHowever, absolute discriminability at the $\\Kempirical$ boundary is scorer-sensitive---alternative scorers reduce Qwen-continuation $\\Kempirical$ from 2 to 1, while rewrite-mode $\\Kempirical = 2$ is preserved (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\nAt 0v1, cross-model $\\auc$ exceeds 0.96 for all pairs, confirming fully transferable binary contamination detection.\n\nA single depth classifier trained on one model's outputs can be deployed for texts from other models without retraining---extending utility to open-web settings where the generating model is unknown.\n\nTwo caveats: (1)~a nontrivial model-specific residual persists despite high transfer, and (2)~Qwen-continuation$\\leftrightarrow$OLMo retention (80--85\\%) is lower than Pythia$\\leftrightarrow$OLMo (90--92\\%), indicating that model family similarity modulates transfer even among continuation-mode configurations.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Cross-scorer validation on Qwen-generated text. Alternative scorers (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B) preserve feature ranking ($\\rho > 0.90$) and rewrite-mode $\\Kempirical = 2$, but reduce continuation-mode $\\Kempirical$ from 2 to 1. $\\dagger$: CI lower bound below $\\theta{=}0.60$. Underlined: $\\auc < \\theta$.}\n\\label{tab:crossscorer}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\input{tables/tab_crossscorer}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Feature Importance Across Decoding Strategies}\n\nWhile $\\Kempirical = 2$ is consistent across decoding strategies, feature importance ordering varies.\nGreedy and $T{=}0.7$ share highly correlated rankings (Spearman $\\rho = 0.914$), with vocabulary diversity measures dominating.\nGreedy versus $T{=}1.2$ yields lower correlation ($\\rho = 0.53$, $p = 0.041$; suggestive but nonsignificant after Bonferroni correction).\n$T{=}1.2$ promotes \\texttt{hapax\\_ratio}, \\texttt{ttr}, and \\texttt{surp\\_skew} to top ranks; \\texttt{freq\\_entropy} rises from rank~10 (greedy) to rank~5.\n\nThe \\emph{existence} of the depth boundary is robust, but the \\emph{optimal feature subset} depends on sampling strategy.\nA general-purpose classifier should incorporate features from multiple groups.\n\n\\subsection{Scale Considerations}\n\nOur $\\Kempirical = 2$ at $\\leq$1.5B constitutes a methodological lower bound.\nAll 15 features are computed from surface-level token statistics without model internals, and cross-model transfer (80--92\\%) demonstrates methodology generalization across architectures.\n\nScaling to larger models presents two outcomes: if $\\Kempirical > 2$ at 7B+ (as scaling laws suggest~\\cite{dohmatob2024model}), depth contamination becomes more insidious and our framework more necessary; if $\\Kempirical = 2$ persists, our conclusions generalize directly.\nThe high cross-model transfer across three architecturally distinct families provides indirect evidence that depth signals are not tied to a specific pretraining paradigm.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Model Neural Probe Analysis}\n\\label{sec:extended_neural_probe}\n",
      "numLines": 60,
      "startLine": 0,
      "totalLines": 152
    }

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01V4NvnbmaHdbjchDLe7qd74`</summary>

```text
0	\section{Discussion}
1	\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\subsection{Why $\Kempirical = 2$: The Information Bottleneck}
4	\label{sec:why_kstar}
5	
6	The shallowness of $\Kempirical = 2$ is explained by the concentration of information loss in the first generation step.
7	The 0v1 boundary is near-trivially separable ($\auc > 0.99$): autoregressive generation systematically compresses the heavy-tailed, bursty surprisal patterns characteristic of human language, even in a single pass.
8	At 1v2, $\auc$ drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp (range: 0.6112--0.7039), indicating that the majority of the remaining discriminative signal is consumed in one additional step.
9	By 2v3, $\auc$ falls below $\theta$ (0.5496--0.5917), and deeper pairs converge toward chance.
10	This rapid convergence reflects the generation operator acting as a lossy channel with diminishing marginal information loss: the first pass removes the most distinguishing structure, and subsequent passes operate on already-smoothed distributions.
11	The consistency of $\Kempirical = 2$ across four decoding strategies (Table~\ref{tab:s2_decoding}) reinforces this interpretation---higher decoding entropy modestly increases signal strength but does not shift the boundary, confirming that $\Kempirical$ is governed by the generation process itself rather than the sampling algorithm.
12	Even at the most marginal condition (Qwen-continuation, where the 95\% CI lower bound exceeds $\theta$ by only 0.15\,pp), $\Kempirical = 2$ survives under self-scoring---suggesting that the boundary is genuine rather than an artifact of generous thresholding.
13	
14	Per-depth analysis provides the most direct evidence that this boundary is fundamental rather than methodological.
15	On a balanced 500-sample test set (Table~\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}), OBD achieves 96\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show a complementary pattern with 69--77\% depth-0 recall and 8--38\% for depths 2--4.
16	OBD achieves nominally higher overall accuracy (38.0\% vs.\ 33.2\%), but the difference is not statistically significant (McNemar exact test: $p = 0.081$ vs.\ GPT-5.5, $p = 0.069$ vs.\ Claude; bootstrap 95\% CIs include zero: $[-0.4, +10.0]$ and $[0.0, +10.2]$\,pp; Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_llm}): the gap originates entirely from depth-0 identification, while both methods fail comparably at depths $\geq 2$.
17	This convergence across fundamentally different approaches---supervised statistical classification and zero-shot frontier LLMs---confirms that the saturation is a property of the data rather than a method limitation.
18	
19	\subsection{The One-Wash Laundering Effect}
```

{

  "parentUuid": "f8e63ba7-414e-46f1-accc-a59e962d721e",

  "promptId": "6513df17-cb00-43d4-897a-3c26ae456c25",

  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/introduction.tex",

    "newString": "across 5 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies at $1.5$--$7$B scale",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nWeb-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.\nEstimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.\nWhen models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.\nThis degradation arises in both \\emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \\emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nYet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically, discarding the generational depth dimension.\n\nThe gap cuts across three communities.\nBinary detection methods~\\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp, zawalski2026codec} produce no depth estimate.\nThe model collapse literature~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} characterizes recursive degradation at the macro level but provides no per-text depth estimation tool.\nData selection methods~\\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} ignore recursive depth.\nNo existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone, nor quantifies the boundary beyond which such estimation becomes infeasible.\n\nThe Data Processing Inequality guarantees that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\\Kstar$.\nWe empirically measure $\\Kempirical$---the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations---using 15 statistical features and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.\nWe operationalize $\\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \\geq 1$ where the bootstrap 95\\% CI for pairwise $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\\theta = 0.60$.\n\nOur contributions center on two empirical findings:\n\\begin{enumerate}[label=(\\alph*),nosep]\n  \\item \\textbf{$\\Kempirical = 2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$} (Cohen's $h{\\approx}0.20$) across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies at ${\\leq}$1.5B scale, with all 1v2 CI lower bounds exceeding $\\theta$ and all 2v3 CI upper bounds below $\\theta$ ($p \\leq 0.002$; Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n  Generation mode is a stronger determinant than model identity ($+$8.4\\,pp rewrite vs.\\ continuation within Qwen; $3.9$\\,pp spread across continuation-mode models).\n  This is independently corroborated: both supervised OBD and zero-shot frontier LLMs fail comparably at depths $\\geq 2$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$), confirming $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as a data property (Section~\\ref{sec:why_kstar}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Model-free cross-model transfer}: 15 statistical features enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention across continuation-mode generators with scorer-invariant feature ordering ($\\rho > 0.90$; Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}), with surprisal-variance features dominating ($\\rho \\geq 0.85$).\n  Cross-scorer validation confirms that $\\Kempirical$ is governed by the generation process rather than the scoring model.\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\noindent As a downstream validation, $\\Kempirical = 2$ correctly predicts the strategy ordering in data filtering: binary curation at the $\\Kempirical$ boundary minimizes degradation most effectively (MMLU $0.5941$ vs.\\ base $0.6018$), while graduated ($0.5892$) and surprisal-based ($0.5894$) strategies provide no additional benefit (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 19,
        "oldLines": 7,
        "newStart": 19,
        "newLines": 7,
        "lines": [
          " ",
          " Our contributions center on two empirical findings:",
          " \\begin{enumerate}[label=(\\alph*),nosep]",
          "-  \\item \\textbf{$\\Kempirical = 2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$} (Cohen's $h{\\approx}0.20$) across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies at ${\\leq}$1.5B scale, with all 1v2 CI lower bounds exceeding $\\theta$ and all 2v3 CI upper bounds below $\\theta$ ($p \\leq 0.002$; Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).",
          "+  \\item \\textbf{$\\Kempirical = 2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$} (Cohen's $h{\\approx}0.20$) across 5 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies at $1.5$--$7$B scale, with all 1v2 CI lower bounds exceeding $\\theta$ and all 2v3 CI upper bounds below $\\theta$ ($p \\leq 0.002$; Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).",
          "   Generation mode is a stronger determinant than model identity ($+$8.4\\,pp rewrite vs.\\ continuation within Qwen; $3.9$\\,pp spread across continuation-mode models).",
          "   This is independently corroborated: both supervised OBD and zero-shot frontier LLMs fail comparably at depths $\\geq 2$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$), confirming $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as a data property (Section~\\ref{sec:why_kstar}).",
          "   \\item \\textbf{Model-free cross-model transfer}: 15 statistical features enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention across continuation-mode generators with scorer-invariant feature ordering ($\\rho > 0.90$; Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}), with surprisal-variance features dominating ($\\rho \\geq 0.85$)."
        ]

    "originalFile": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}\n\\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\n\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nA generation operator $G_\\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\\delta$) produces successive generations $X \\to Y_1 \\to \\cdots \\to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \\triangleq X$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nThis sequence forms a Markov chain.\nBy the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \\geq I(X; Y_2) \\geq \\cdots \\geq 0$: the original signal degrades monotonically.\nTogether with Markov chain convergence, this guarantees a finite $\\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations are indistinguishable.\n\nWe define the operational lower bound:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\Kempirical = \\max\\{k \\geq 1 : \\text{CI}_{\\text{lower}}^{95\\%}(\\auc_{k-1,k}) > \\theta\\},\n  \\label{eq:kstar}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\theta = 0.60$ corresponds to Cohen's $h \\approx 0.20$\\footnote{$h = 2\\arcsin\\!\\sqrt{0.60} - 2\\arcsin\\!\\sqrt{0.50} \\approx 0.2014$, a ``small'' effect size by Cohen's (1988) convention.} (a conventional small-effect-size threshold) and lies well above the permutation null distribution (95th percentile $< 0.55$ across all configurations; bootstrap: 1{,}000 resamples).\nThis choice is further validated by the downstream filtering experiment (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}), where $\\theta{=}0.60$ captures the practically meaningful boundary between actionable and non-actionable depth distinctions.\n$\\Kempirical$ varies with $\\theta$: at $\\theta{=}0.55$, $\\Kempirical{=}2$--$3$ depending on configuration ($\\Kempirical{=}3$ for Qwen variants, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for Pythia and OLMo); at $\\theta{=}0.65$, continuation modes drop to $\\Kempirical{=}1$; at $\\theta{=}0.70$, $\\Kempirical{=}1$ universally (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n\n\\subsection{Features and Classifier}\n\\label{sec:features}\n\nEach document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \\emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \\emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \\emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio, hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \\emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).\nAll features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (full definitions in Table~\\ref{tab:s5_features}, Appendix).\nHand-crafted features enable cross-generator transfer without retraining and permit attribution of discriminative power to specific text properties.\n\nWe use \\emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\\text{depth} > k \\mid \\mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM (hyperparameters in Table~\\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}).\nOBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:experimental_setup}\n\nWe evaluate four configurations spanning three model families (Table~\\ref{tab:setup}): Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation modes), Pythia-1.4B and OLMo-1B (continuation).\nEach uses 5{,}000 C4 seeds truncated to 256 tokens, recursively generated for $K{=}5$ iterations (30{,}000 documents per configuration).\nDefault decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; decoding ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.\nContinuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.\nEvaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\\auc$ as primary metric.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Experimental configurations.}\n\\label{tab:setup}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nPythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nOLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n",
